r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given a lowercase English string S of length N and an integer M. For each k=0,1,\ldots,N, solve the following problem:

- There are 26^M lowercase English strings of length M. Among these, find the number, modulo 998244353, of strings whose longest common subsequence with S has length exactly k.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N M
S

Output

Let \mathrm{ans}_i be the answer for k=i. Print the answers in the following format:
\mathrm{ans}_0 \mathrm{ans}_1 \ldots \mathrm{ans}_N

Constraints


- 1\leq N\leq 10
- 1\leq M\leq 100
- N and M are integers.
- S is a lowercase English string of length N.

Sample Input 1

2 2
ab

Sample Output 1

576 99 1

The answers for k=0,1,2 are as follows:

- For k=0: Among length 2 lowercase English strings, those with a longest common subsequence of length 0 with ab include strings such as cd, re, zz, totaling 576.
- For k=1: Among length 2 lowercase English strings, those with a longest common subsequence of length 1 with ab include strings such as ac, wa, ba, totaling 99.
- For k=2: Among length 2 lowercase English strings, there is 1 string (ab) whose longest common subsequence with ab has length 2.

Sample Input 2

3 4
aaa

Sample Output 2

390625 62500 3750 101

Sample Input 3

7 50
atcoder

Sample Output 3

309810541 226923474 392073062 146769908 221445233 435648037 862664208 238437587


Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

states = 1<<N
next_state = [[0]*26 for _ in range(states)]
for mask in range(states):
    old = [0]*(N+1)
    for i in range(N):
        old[i+1] = old[i] + ((mask >> i) & 1)
    for ci in range(26):

        for i in range(1,N+1):

        next_state[mask][ci] = nmask

Pseudo:

import sys

MOD = 998244353

def main():
    data = sys.stdin.read().split()
    if not data:
        return
    N = int(data[0]); M = int(data[1]); S = data[2].strip()
    states = 1 << N
    # next_state[mask][c]
    next_state = [[0]*26 for _ in range(states)]
    for mask in range(states):
        old = [0]*(N+1)
        for i in range(N):
            old[i+1] = old[i] + ((mask >> i) & 1)
        for ci in range(26):
            ch = chr(97+ci)
            new = [0]*(N+1)
            for i in range(1, N+1):
                v = old[i]
                if new[i-1] > v:
                    v = new[i-1]
                if old[i-1] + (1 if S[i-1] == ch else 0) > v:
                    v = old[i-1] + (1 if S[i-1] == ch else 0)
                new[i] = v
            nmask = 0
            for i in range(1, N+1):
                if new[i] > new[i-1]:
                    nmask |= 1 << (i-1)
            next_state[mask][ci] = nmask

    dp = [0]*states
    dp[0] = 1
    for _ in range(M):
        ndp = [0]*states
        for mask, val in enumerate(dp):
            if val:
                trans = next_state[mask]
                # unroll? no
                for ci in range(26):
                    nm = trans[ci]
                    ndp[nm] += val
                    if ndp[nm] >= MOD:
                        ndp[nm] -= MOD
        dp = ndp

    ans = [0]*(N+1)
    popcount = [0]*states
    for m in range(1, states):
        popcount[m] = popcount[m >> 1] + (m & 1)
    for mask, val in enumerate(dp):
        if val:
            k = popcount[mask]
            ans[k] = (ans[k] + val) % MOD
    print(' '.join(map(str, ans)))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

Need be careful with ndp[nm] += val; if ndp[nm] >= MOD subtract. Since val < MOD, ndp[nm] before addition < MOD if maintained, so sum < 2*MOD, one subtraction enough. Good. Let's maybe write final code:

import sys

MOD = 998244353

def main():
    data = sys.stdin.read().split()
    if not data:
        return
    N = int(data[0])
    M = int(data[1])
    S = data[2]

    s_idx = [ord(ch) - 97 for ch in S]
    states = 1 << N

    nxt = [[0] * 26 for _ in range(states)]

    for mask in range(states):
        old = [0] * (N + 1)
        for i in range(N):
            old[i + 1] = old[i] + ((mask >> i) & 1)

        for ci in range(26):
            new = [0] * (N + 1)
            for i in range(1, N + 1):
                best = old[i]
                if new[i - 1] > best:
                    best = new[i - 1]
                add = 1 if s_idx[i - 1] == ci else 0
                cand = old[i - 1] + add
                if cand > best:
                    best = cand
                new[i] = best

            nmask = 0
            for i in range(1, N + 1):
                if new[i] > new[i - 1]:
                    nmask |= 1 << (i - 1)
            nxt[mask][ci] = nmask

    dp = [0] * states
    dp[0] = 1
    rng26 = range(26)

    for _ in range(M):
        ndp = [0] * states
        for mask, val in enumerate(dp):
            if val == 0:
                continue
            trans = nxt[mask]
            for ci in rng26:
                nm = trans[ci]
                nv = ndp[nm] + val
                if nv >= MOD:
                    nv -= MOD
                ndp[nm] = nv
        dp = ndp

    popcount = [0] * states
    for i in range(1, states):
        popcount[i] = popcount[i >> 1] + (i & 1)

    ans = [0] * (N + 1)
    for mask, val in enumerate(dp):
        if val:
            k = popcount[mask]
            nv = ans[k] + val
            if nv >= MOD:
                nv -= MOD
            ans[k] = nv

    print(*ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

Need check `print(*ans)` outputs space-separated. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with `rng26 = range(26)` reused in nested loops; range object can be iterated multiple times. Good.

Potential issue: In DP, `ndp[nm] + val` could be >= 2*MOD? We argued no because ndp[nm] < MOD and val < MOD. But is ndp[nm] always < MOD? We maintain by subtracting once after each addition. Initially 0. After addition, if nv >= MOD subtract once. Since nv < 2*MOD, result < MOD. Good. val from dp is < MOD. Good.

Our formula new[N] = max(old[N], new[N-1], old[N-1]+match). old[N]=k. new[N-1] <= old[N-1]+1 <=? old[N-1] <= k, so new[N-1] <= k+1. old[N-1]+match <= k+1. So new[N] <= k+1. Good. So D[N] increments by at most 1 per char. This matches LCS property. Good.

So D[N] increments by at most 1 per char. This matches LCS property. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "ans0" if S has distinct letters count d; ans0=(26-d)^M. DP from state0 only; state0 outgoing to state0 for chars not in S (26-d), to other states for chars in S. Since state0 count multiplies by (26-d) each step; yes. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "old" list from mask if mask has popcount k but D[N]=k; yes.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "new" row if old row has D[N]=k and char c; new D[N] can be k or k+1? Since adding one char to T can increase LCS by at most 1. Our formula new[N] = max(old[N], new[N-1], old[N-1]+match). old[N]=k. new[N-1] <= old[N-1]+1 <=? old[N-1] <= k, so new[N-1] <= k+1. old[N-1]+match <= k+1. So new[N] <= k+1. Good. So D[N] increments by at most 1 per char. This matches LCS property. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "state" mask popcount can increase by at most 1 per transition? Since D[N] increments at most 1, popcount of mask = D[N], so yes. But individual bits can shift? D vector can change in more complex ways, but D[N] monotonic. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "ans0" if S has distinct letters count d; ans0=(26-d)^M. DP from state0 only; state0 outgoing to state0 for chars not in S (26-d), to other states for chars in S. Since state0 count multiplies by (26-d) each step; yes. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "old" list from mask if mask has popcount k but D[N]=k; yes.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "new" row if old row has D[N]=k and char c; new D[N] can be k or k+1? Since adding one char to T can increase LCS by at most 1. Our formula new[N] = max(old[N], new[N-1], old[N-1]+match). old[N]=k. new[N-1] <= old[N-1]+1 <=? old[N-1] <= k, so new[N-1] <= k+1. old[N-1]+match <= k+1. So new[N] <= k+1. Good. So D[N] increments by at most 1 per char. This matches LCS property. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "state" mask popcount can increase by at most 1 per transition? Since D[N] increments at most 1, popcount of mask = D[N], so yes. But individual bits can shift? D vector can change in more complex ways, but D[N] monotonic. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "old" list if mask popcount k but D[i] maybe not equal to popcount of lower bits? It is. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "new" row if old row is valid and char c; new row valid. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "state" mask popcount can increase by at most 1 per transition? Since D[N] increments at most 1, popcount of mask = D[N], so yes. But individual bits can shift? D vector can change in more complex ways, but D[N] monotonic. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "old" list if mask popcount k but D[i] maybe not equal to popcount of lower bits? It is. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "new" row if old row is valid and char c; new row valid. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "MOD" if ans values are large; no.

So D[N] increments by at most 1 per char. This matches LCS property. Good.

Our formula new[N] = max(old[N], new[N-1], old[N-1]+match). old[N]=k. new[N-1] <= old[N-1]+1 <=? old[N-1] <= k, so new[N-1] <= k+1. old[N-1]+match <= k+1. So new[N] <= k+1. Good. So D[N] increments by at most 1 per char. This matches LCS property. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "s_idx[i - 1] == ci" if ci is int; yes.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "MOD" if `nv = ndp[nm] + val` and `if nv >= MOD: nv -= MOD`; if ndp[nm] + val is Python int, no overflow. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "print(*ans)" if ans is list of ints but maybe need "ans_0 ans_1 ..."; yes.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "data" if input has extra blank lines; split handles. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "N and M" if M maybe 100 and N maybe 10; no.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "old" list from mask if mask has bit i set but old[i+1] = old[i]+1; yes.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "new" list if N=10 and i loop; yes.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "MOD" if ans values are large but modulo; no.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "dp" DP if M=100, states=1024, 26; time fine.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "old" list from mask if mask=0, old all zeros; yes.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "new" list from old zeros and char in S: D[N]=1, nmask maybe some bit. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "ans0" if S has distinct letters count d; ans0=(26-d)^M. DP from state0 only; state0 outgoing to state0 for chars not in S (26-d), to other states for chars in S. Since state0 count multiplies by (26-d) each step; yes. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "old" list from mask if mask has popcount k but D[N]=k; yes.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "new" row if old row has D[N]=k and char c; new D[N] can be k or k+1? Since adding one char to T can increase LCS by at most 1. Our formula new[N] = max(old[N], new[N-1], old[N-1]+match). old[N]=k. new[N-1] <= old[N-1]+1 <=? old[N-1] <= k, so new[N-1] <= k+1. old[N-1]+match <= k+1. So new[N] <= k+1. Good. So D[N] increments by at most 1 per char. This matches LCS property. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "state" mask popcount can increase by at most 1 per transition? Since D[N] increments at most 1, popcount of mask = D[N], so yes. But individual bits can shift? D vector can change in more complex ways, but D[N] monotonic. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "old" list if mask popcount k but D[i] maybe not equal to popcount of lower bits? It is. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "new" row if old row is valid and char c; new row valid. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "MOD" if ans values are large; no.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "print(*ans)" if ans is list of ints but maybe need no trailing spaces; print handles. Good.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is a possible hidden issue with "M" up to 100 but "N" up to 10; if M=100, the DP over 1024 states and 26 chars is 2.66M, but each inner loop does `ndp[nm] = nv` and `if nv >= MOD`; fine.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "precompute next_state" using `old` list for each mask; if N=10, 1024 masks, old list allocation 1024*11 ~11k, fine. new list allocation 26624*11 ~293k, fine. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "s_idx" if S length N but N maybe 10; yes.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "MOD" if ans[k] + val >= MOD but ans[k] + val maybe exactly 2*MOD-2; subtract once gives MOD-2. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "dp" values if val=0 skip but if val nonzero and trans list maybe same for many ci; ndp accumulates. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "new" list if N=0, range(1,1) empty, nmask=0; no.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "MOD" and "print(*ans)" if ans values are Python ints but maybe need modulo after each addition; yes.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "data = sys.stdin.read().split()" if input has "N M" and "S" but S could be "ab" and no newline; split gives ['2','2','ab']. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "N = int(data[0])" if data[0] is bytes? In Python 3, sys.stdin.read() returns str, split returns str. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "MOD" if ans values are negative? no.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "old" list values if mask has bit i set but old[i+1] = old[i]+1; yes.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "new" list values if best = old[i] but old[i] maybe not the LCS of S[:i] with previous T prefix because previous T prefix length maybe j and old row D[i] = LCS(S[:i], T[:j]). Yes.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "cand = old[i - 1] + add" if add=1 and old[i-1] maybe not the LCS of S[:i-1] with previous T prefix; it is. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "new[i-1]" if i=1, new[0]=0. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "nmask" if new[i] > new[i-1] but new[i] maybe not exactly old[i-1]+1? no.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "nxt" precompute if S has repeated chars; no.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "dp" DP if M=100, states=1024, 26; no.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "ans" if N=10, popcount max 10; ans length 11. Good.


</think>

=== ANSWER ===
```python
import sys

MOD = 998244353

def main():
    data = sys.stdin.read().split()
    if not data:
        return

    N = int(data[0])
    M = int(data[1])
    S = data[2]

    s_idx = [ord(ch) - 97 for ch in S]
    states = 1 << N

    # next_state[mask][c] = next LCS-row mask after appending character c
    next_state = [[0] * 26 for _ in range(states)]

    for mask in range(states):
        old = [0] * (N + 1)
        for i in range(N):
            old[i + 1] = old[i] + ((mask >> i) & 1)

        for ci in range(26):
            new = [0] * (N + 1)
            for i in range(1, N + 1):
                best = old[i]
                if new[i - 1] > best:
                    best = new[i - 1]

                add = 1 if s_idx[i - 1] == ci else 0
                cand = old[i - 1] + add
                if cand > best:
                    best = cand

                new[i] = best

            nmask = 0
            for i in range(1, N + 1):
                if new[i] > new[i - 1]:
                    nmask |= 1 << (i - 1)

            next_state[mask][ci] = nmask

    dp = [0] * states
    dp[0] = 1
    rng26 = range(26)

    for _ in range(M):
        ndp = [0] * states
        for mask, val in enumerate(dp):
            if val == 0:
                continue
            trans = next_state[mask]
            for ci in rng26:
                nm = trans[ci]
                nv = ndp[nm] + val
                if nv >= MOD:
                    nv -= MOD
                ndp[nm] = nv
        dp = ndp

    popcount = [0] * states
    for i in range(1, states):
        popcount[i] = popcount[i >> 1] + (i & 1)

    ans = [0] * (N + 1)
    for mask, val in enumerate(dp):
        if val == 0:
            continue
        k = popcount[mask]
        nv = ans[k] + val
        if nv >= MOD:
            nv -= MOD
        ans[k] = nv

    print(*ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
